16:I[44692,["/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/08cykig6vtx_p.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/07qi2okp-.yp9.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0frujd.eyhz4o.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0n4z.7yu76je-.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/09-o.77-p0p36.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/1304s0wcp2kce.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0j1uy9t~zxcf0.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/14_5o-nbg8k_r.js"],"DailyView"]
17:T55f,There are three main technical moves. First, the paper formalizes a subgroup-targeted membership inference game. Targets are sampled from a designated pool, while the rest of the private dataset is sampled from its complement. This makes it possible to audit high-risk groups, random controls, or unions of groups under a common reference distribution. Second, the authors adapt normalized-evidence membership inference to synthetic text releases. The exact likelihood-ratio test over an entire released synthetic dataset is intractable, so they replace it with measurable proxy evidence extracted from the release, such as n-gram overlap and embedding-based distances. These proxies are then normalized through scenario-dependent calibration. The separation is important: evidence comes from the release itself, while calibration captures attacker knowledge. Third, attacker capability is modeled explicitly through three release-based scenarios: release-only, raw-reference, and generator-assisted release. This lets the audit distinguish what can be inferred from the released samples alone, what auxiliary reference data adds, and how much stronger attacks become if the generator is also available. The paper also goes beyond pool-level metrics by estimating per-record vulnerability scores from repeated attack episodes and relating them to intrinsic record attributes.18:T522,The study spans four datasets, three DP text generators, five privacy budgets, and 32 proxy attacks. The main empirical result is not simply that DP helps, but that it helps unevenly. For high-risk records under non-private generators, the strongest attacker reaches AUC 0.79–0.83, with 34–61% true-positive rate at under 5% false-positive rate. Even a release-only attacker can recover 39–52% of true members in that setting, which means the released synthetic text alone can carry substantial membership evidence. Under DP, average AUC drops substantially, but leakage remains above chance at every tested privacy budget: AUC stays around 0.55–0.65, with TPR up to about 18% at 5% FPR. The more important pattern is heterogeneity. High-risk pools remain more distinguishable than random controls under every DP budget, so DP does not flatten risk uniformly across groups. Within a pool, leakage is also concentrated: roughly 10% of records account for about 40% of total leakage. That concentration is the most actionable finding, because it suggests that identifying and treating vulnerable records may matter as much as tightening epsilon. The paper also compares mechanisms, proxies, and attacker scenarios, showing that residual leakage depends on both the data distribution and the synthesis method.19:T933,技术路线可以拆成六块。1）架构：采用统一的高稀疏细粒度 MoE，从 16B 到 1T 都维持 256 routed experts、每 token 激活 8 个 routed expert 加 1 个 shared expert，激活比例约 3.5%。这种设计不是追求极端稀疏本身，而是为了在固定 active FLOPs 下扩大总容量，并保持跨尺度结构一致性，便于 scaling law 外推。文中还配合 MTP（multi-token prediction）提升训练效率和推理相关能力，并使用 aux-loss-free load balancing，避免传统负载均衡辅助损失对主目标的干扰。2）Scaling law：作者声称基于上千组实验建立 Ling Scaling Laws，把它当作“风洞”系统，用低成本小实验预测超大规模模型的最优超参和收益区间，验证成本低于完整训练的 1%。这部分的关键不是公式新颖，而是把架构稀疏度、active compute、训练稳定性和最终能力放进同一套经验外推框架。3）预训练数据：构造 reasoning-oriented corpus，重点提高 Ling Math 与 Ling Code 的占比，并在 20T token 训练过程中把 reasoning data 比例从 32% 提到 46%。这说明作者不是把数学/代码当作后期补药，而是把其视为基础能力塑形信号。4）Mid-training：在预训练后、对齐前插入 reasoning pre-activation 阶段，延长有效上下文并引入 CoT 数据，目标是先把模型内部表征调到更适合多步推理的区域，再进入 SFT/RL。这个设计隐含一个判断：如果 base model 没有被预激活，后续 RL 往往更不稳定，也更依赖高质量长推理轨迹。5）后训练：先用 DFT（Decoupled Fine-Tuning）和差异化 system prompt 做多样化初始化，再用 Evo-CoT 逐步加深推理链复杂度；RL 阶段提出 LPO，把句子而非 token 或整段序列作为 policy optimization 的 action unit，以改善 credit assignment 与训练稳定性；偏好对齐阶段用 GAR 做组内比较，试图更细地拟合开放式回答中的相对偏好。6）系统：全程 FP8 训练，量化粒度为 activation/gradient [1,128]、weight [128,128]，并配合异构细粒度 pipeline、interleaved 1F1B 与 partial recomputation，处理 MTP 和 First-K-Dense 等异构模块带来的 bubble 问题。这里的重点是系统设计直接服务于预训练范式，而不是单独追求吞吐数字。1a:T92f,The method has six major components. 1) Architecture: a unified high-sparsity fine-grained MoE family from 16B to 1T, with 256 routed experts and 8 routed + 1 shared expert activated per token, or roughly 3.5% activation. The point is not sparsity for its own sake, but larger total capacity under fixed active FLOPs while keeping the architecture consistent across scales. MTP is added to improve efficiency and reasoning-related performance, and load balancing is handled without an auxiliary loss to avoid interference with the main objective. 2) Scaling laws: the Ling Scaling Laws are built from more than a thousand experiments and used as a low-cost “wind tunnel” for architecture and hyperparameter selection. The claimed contribution is not a new closed-form law, but an empirical framework that jointly tracks sparsity, active compute, stability, and final capability. 3) Pretraining data: the corpus is explicitly reasoning-oriented, with Ling Math and Ling Code emphasized and the reasoning-data ratio increased from 32% to 46% over 20T tokens. This treats reasoning data as a shaping signal for the base model, not merely a late-stage booster. 4) Mid-training: the paper inserts a reasoning pre-activation stage between pretraining and alignment, extending effective context length and introducing CoT data. The implicit claim is that RL works better when the base model has already moved into a representation regime suitable for multi-step reasoning. 5) Post-training: DFT with differentiated system prompts is used to create a diverse reasoning-focused initialization, followed by Evo-CoT to progressively deepen reasoning trajectories. In RL, LPO defines sentences rather than tokens or full sequences as the action unit, aiming for better credit assignment and stability. Preference alignment uses GAR, a group-based reward mechanism for finer-grained intra-group comparisons in open-ended outputs. 6) Systems: the full training stack runs in FP8, with activation/gradient quantization at [1,128] and weight quantization at [128,128]. This is paired with heterogeneous fine-grained pipeline scheduling, interleaved 1F1B, and partial recomputation to reduce bubbles caused by modules such as MTP and First-K-Dense. The systems work is tightly tied to the training recipe rather than presented as standalone throughput engineering.1b:T583,The paper reports a broad set of quantitative results. The model family spans 16B through intermediate scales up to 1T total parameters under a unified sparse MoE design. With roughly 3.5% parameter activation per token, the authors claim up to 7x active-compute efficiency relative to dense counterparts. Pretraining uses 20T tokens, and increasing the reasoning-oriented data ratio from 32% to 46% yields average gains of 5% to 8% on reasoning benchmarks. The WSM scheduler improves average benchmark performance by 1% to 2% over conventional learning-rate decay, and the gain survives later post-training. DFT plus Evo-CoT reportedly surpasses comparison models on competition-level math reasoning while requiring 25% fewer training tokens to reach comparable or better performance. LPO improves complex reasoning benchmarks by around 10% over token-level and sequence-level RL baselines. GAR improves consistency scores in open-ended evaluation by 2% to 10%. On the systems side, full FP8 training stays within 0.25% of BF16 after 900B tokens while reducing memory usage by more than 15%, and the heterogeneous pipeline design improves throughput by about 40%. The most transferable results are the data curriculum, WSM, LPO, and FP8 findings, because they map to four different layers of the stack: pretraining, the pretrain-to-posttrain interface, RL credit assignment, and large-scale training stability.1c:T5a8,Read it in layers. Start with the introduction and the overview figures to see how the authors decompose gains into architecture, data, mid-training, post-training, and infrastructure, instead of attributing everything to scale. Then go to the Ling Scaling Laws section and inspect how active compute is defined, how small-scale runs are extrapolated to 1T, and which hyperparameters are claimed to remain stable across scales. That section determines whether the work is a reusable recipe or a one-off engineering stack. Next, read the pretraining data composition and mid-training sections carefully: focus on how the 20T-token curriculum is scheduled, when long-context and CoT data are introduced, and why those choices raise the ceiling for later RL. After that, study the ablations for WSM, DFT/Evo-CoT, LPO, and GAR. The key is not the names but the failure modes they target: inefficient late-stage LR decay, narrow initialization, overly fragmented token-level credit assignment, and preference signals that are too coarse. Finish with the systems sections, especially the FP8 configuration and heterogeneous pipeline scheduling, because those details determine whether the recipe is actually deployable at scale. If time is limited, prioritize the scaling-law figures, data-mixture ablations, WSM comparisons, LPO comparisons, and the FP8-vs-BF16 loss gap curves. That should be enough to capture the main ideas in about 60 to 90 minutes.1d:T41b,The analysis starts from a standard sparse MoE layer: the router scores all experts, selects the top-k experts, re-normalizes the selected scores, and forms a weighted sum of expert outputs. Under mild assumptions that activated experts have comparable output norms and weak pairwise correlations, the RMS of the MoE output is largely governed by the sum of squared normalized routing weights. Reducing k removes lower-scored experts and re-normalizes the remaining weights, increasing this squared-weight concentration. This explains why smaller k amplifies the RMS and variance of the MoE output. To correct the mismatch, the paper proposes Layer-wise Distribution Alignment (LDA). LDA applies a per-dimension moment correction after each SMoE layer, aligning representations produced under reduced expert activation with reference statistics collected from the default top-k0 routing configuration. It requires no retraining or architectural change and can be combined with static top-k reduction, adaptive top-p routing, or dynamic expert pruning.1e:T4b4,EFQ-Softmax directly converts shifted attention scores into block-scaled E2M1 probability operands, bypassing the usual exp-then-quantize pipeline. “Exp-Free” is partial rather than absolute: dense elementwise exponentials for the current score block are removed, but the row-level exponential terms needed for historical rescaling in online attention are retained. The method works blockwise. For each microscaling block, it selects an exponent-only scale from the local maximum, maps shifted scores into a scale-normalized residual domain, and generates nonnegative E2M1 codes using a single affine index rule. Together with a block-level E8M0 scale, these codes form the final MXFP4 probability operand. The same approximation is used consistently for both numerator and denominator updates, which is important because softmax quality depends on matching those two paths. A key strength of the design is that it does not merely approximate exp as a scalar function; it rewrites the intermediate representation so it is directly consumable by low-bit PV multiplication while preserving row-max updates, historical rescaling, high-precision accumulation, and final normalization in online attention.1f:T408,The evaluation covers Qwen3-8B, Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct, and WAN2.2-TI2V-5B, so the method is tested on text, vision-language, and video generation workloads. On Qwen3-8B, the seven-task mean score improves from 0.6749 with the MXFP4 baseline to 0.6773. The gain is small, but it shows that direct score-to-E2M1 generation does not degrade model quality. On Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct, the nine-task mean rises from 0.7826 to 0.8000, and all six referring-expression grounding splits improve, which is a more noticeable result. On WAN2.2, VBench indicates temporal consistency and visual quality comparable to FP16 and MXFP4 baselines. The abstract emphasizes quality retention more than raw speed numbers, so the full paper should be checked for kernel-level latency, throughput, memory-traffic reduction, and sensitivity to block size and scaling choices. The practical claim is that removing dense exp and the temporary high-precision probability tile should reduce overhead in the softmax path once GEMMs are already low-bit accelerated.20:T4bd,The approach is an instruction-centric, train-and-measure loop:
- Search space: expose ARM Neon primitives as trainable operations spanning FP16 residual/normalization, INT8 SDOT for attention projections, and bit-packed boolean compute using compare/route/logic ops plus popcount.
- Dataflow: keep the residual stream in FP16; use FCMGE (FP compare) with learned thresholds to convert channels into packed bits; run a learned boolean feed-forward “program” implemented via EXT/BSL/EOR/CNT; decode selected boolean traces back to numeric space via CNT/SDOT and apply small FP corrections (FMLA).
- Train/deploy alignment: this is not post-training quantization. Thresholds and the boolean program are trained jointly; FP16 rounding and hard bit decisions are present during training. Gradients use a straight-through/hybrid estimator, but inference executes the exact hard boolean program.
- Selection protocol: for each primitive/variant, train on fixed data order and seed, inspect validation curves, export the exact native weight image, and benchmark bytes and tokens/s on an M4. Variants that fail either quality or speed/memory gates are dropped; a 3-seed calibration is used to estimate endpoint noise.21:T82d,ConvMem reformulates long-context QA as hierarchical convolution. Given a query Q and a very long document D, the model splits D into windows of size W. A frozen LLM, prompted with the query and a local chunk, acts as a semantic convolution kernel that extracts a chunk-level summary, evidence, or intermediate memory. All chunks at the same level are processed in parallel. Their outputs are then grouped and recursively summarized in higher layers, producing a tree of aggregations until a final global memory is formed for answer generation. Compared with sequential memory agents, the reasoning path becomes logarithmic in depth rather than linear in the number of chunks, while parallelism increases within each layer.

The paper adds three mechanisms on top of this backbone. First, configurable strides: using stride S < W creates overlapping windows, so the same evidence can be seen from multiple receptive fields. This is meant to reduce boundary truncation and improve recall when evidence spans chunk boundaries. Second, semantic skip connections: because hierarchical summarization can discard fine-grained details, high-confidence raw evidence is allowed to bypass intermediate compression stages and flow directly into the final reasoning stage. Third, multi-kernel convolution: the original query is decomposed into sub-questions, and separate semantic channels are used to extract evidence for different reasoning threads before merging them. This is intended to reduce interference between orthogonal subproblems.

Methodologically, ConvMem is a prompt-and-orchestrate framework rather than a newly trained model. It depends on the base LLM’s existing abilities in summarization, evidence extraction, and QA. Relative to MemAgent-style systems, the main differences are: no learned memory policy, tree-structured rather than chain-structured information flow, and parallel chunk processing instead of strictly sequential reading. Relative to standard retrieval pipelines, it is closer to full-document hierarchical coverage than sparse retrieval over a few selected chunks.22:T678,The evaluation is centered on long-context multi-hop QA settings, notably RULER-HotpotQA and RULER-2WikiMultiHopQA. The main empirical message has three parts. First, ConvMem outperforms training-free baselines, suggesting that hierarchical parallel aggregation is more effective than one-shot summarization, naive chunked QA, or simple map-reduce style prompting for multi-hop evidence integration. Second, ConvMem generalizes better than RL-trained memory agents on out-of-distribution settings. The authors emphasize that RL-based agents may look strong in-distribution but degrade more sharply under distribution shift and can produce plausible answers not actually grounded in the input. Third, the three architectural components each contribute: overlapping strides improve evidence recall, skip connections help preserve details, and multi-kernel decomposition is more useful on semantically entangled questions.

The most interesting part of the experiments is the failure-mode analysis rather than a single headline score. The paper explicitly studies overfitting in RL memory agents and argues that these systems can exploit pretrained knowledge or benchmark regularities instead of following a faithful memory trajectory over the document. Because ConvMem does not train a policy, it avoids some of these benchmark-specific shortcuts. The provided excerpt does not include the full result tables, so I will not invent exact numbers. But the structure of the experiments makes the intended claim clear: the gain is less about maximizing one benchmark score and more about improving parallelism, robustness, and grounding under distribution shift.23:T4d9,This is a skim paper, and it is best read for the design idea rather than for formal novelty. Start with the introduction to understand what it is replacing: not long-attention models, but RL-based memory agents. Then read the method section around the definitions of W, S, L, and C, plus the prompt design for the semantic kernel. That is where the CNN analogy becomes operational rather than rhetorical. After that, focus on the three mechanisms: overlapping strides, semantic skip connections, and multi-kernel channels. Each one maps to a concrete long-context failure mode: boundary truncation, detail loss under compression, and interference between reasoning threads.

In the experiments, prioritize the OOD results and the case studies on RL overfitting instead of only reading the main table. A 30–40 minute pass is enough to capture the method and the main empirical claim. If reading more deeply, three follow-up questions are worth keeping in mind: how much of the gain comes from structure versus prompt engineering, whether tree aggregation compresses away critical intermediate variables on harder reasoning chains, and whether a learned selector or verifier could be added without reintroducing the same overfitting issues.24:T818,The paper has two technical components. The first is a mechanistic diagnosis of what the authors call the consensus–termination gap. They generate one main reasoning trajectory per problem, freeze it, and then append probes offline to different prefixes of that same trajectory. This frozen-trajectory design is crucial because it ensures that different probing schedules and stopping rules observe identical underlying reasoning rather than perturbing it. On top of this substrate, they run three direct measurements.

First, wording sensitivity: for the same frozen prefix, they vary the wording of the probe and check whether the extracted current answer changes. If early prefixes are highly sensitive to probe wording, then the answer is being elicited by the query rather than read out from a settled internal state. Second, a hand-labeled taxonomy of wrong stops: among cases where the rule stops and the committed answer is wrong, they distinguish trajectories that had genuinely converged to a wrong answer from trajectories that had not converged at all and were merely forced into a temporary placeholder by the probe. Third, non-terminal commitment analysis: they measure how often a consensus stop commits to an answer that the original trajectory later abandons, and whether the truncated continuation would have corrected that answer.

The second component is a preregistered exhaustive sweep over a family of windowed consensus rules. The search space includes window size, agreement-share threshold, and operational knobs, for a total of 3,520 rules. These are evaluated on frozen trajectories from two models and three benchmarks with problem-grouped splits and pre-fixed acceptance gates. This is a strong design choice: instead of showcasing a few hand-picked operating points, the paper asks whether the rule family contains any robustly usable point at all. A non-consensus baseline, DEER, is run through the exact same pipeline and token accounting, which isolates whether the failure comes from the signal or from the early-exit objective itself.25:T858,The empirical case is quite tight. On the mechanism side, re-probing the same frozen prefix with different wording yields different answers 54% of the time in the first tenth of a trajectory, versus 16% near the end. Early extracted answers are therefore highly probe-dependent. In a hand-labeled set of 134 wrong-stop cases, more than half are situations where the model had not actually converged and the probe forced a premature answer, while only about a quarter are cases where the model had truly settled on a wrong answer. The non-terminal commitment analysis is even more telling: at a consensus rule that still saves 32% of tokens, one stop in nine commits an answer that the trajectory later leaves behind. Among 216 such non-terminal stops, 155 cut off a correction the trajectory would have made, while only 16 banked a correct answer from a trajectory that would later end wrong. In other words, the computation being removed is mostly useful corrective computation.

The rule sweep then shows that this is not a threshold artifact. Across 3,520 preregistered consensus rules, none clears the pre-specified safety-and-savings gates on development, so none is carried forward. Yet 478 rules clear a gate in-sample on the training split, which explains how a less disciplined selection protocol could have produced optimistic conclusions. The frontier reproduces strongly on the held-out test split (r = 0.98) and on unseen models, including one at 4× scale and with a different architecture, so the negative result is stable. The contrast with DEER is the key control: under the same protocol, DEER clears all three gates and on unseen models can lose as little as 0.3 percentage points while saving 28% of tokens. At matched savings, consensus is much more expensive: at 28% net saving, the cheapest consensus rule still costs 6.0 points of accuracy, and at 33% saving it costs 8.3 points, versus 0.5 and 2.0 points for DEER. The paper also notes that a shipped operating point, CertaIndex (CoT), loses 56–70 points on the same trajectories, which underscores how brittle these heuristics can be under strict accounting.26:T492,Although labeled skim, this deserves a careful skim if you work on reasoning inference. Start with the introduction to pin down the target: this is not self-consistency across independently sampled trajectories, but repeated probing of a single partial trajectory. Then go straight to Section 4, which contains the strongest evidence. The three pieces to focus on are probe-wording sensitivity, the wrong-stop taxonomy, and the non-terminal commitment analysis. Together they support the central claim that agreement only shows persistence under a fixed elicitation procedure, not reasoning termination. After that, read Section 5 for the preregistered sweep design: acceptance gates, problem-grouped splits, frozen-trajectory replay, and token accounting are what make the negative result credible.

If time is short, prioritize Figure 1, the matched-savings accuracy comparisons around 28% and 33%, and the key counts 54% versus 16% and 155 versus 16. About 30 minutes is enough to get the main argument. If you are building early-exit systems, verifiers, or reasoning-boundary detectors, the protocol details in the experiments and appendix are worth reading in full.27:T79a,The method has three layers. First is IRT calibration. The authors fit a Two-Parameter Logistic model to the binary response matrix of 1,000 open-weight models on the full MMLU test set. They use stochastic variational inference in NumPyro/JAX rather than joint maximum likelihood, explicitly to avoid the incidental-parameters problem, since model ability parameters grow with sample size and can make item estimates inconsistent under JML. This yields item difficulty b_i, item discrimination a_i, and model ability theta_j. Second is explanatory modeling of difficulty. The paper builds a deterministic, text-extractable framework of structural features intended to separate declarative retrieval demand from procedural reasoning load. The named features include Zipf Rarity, Entity Density, and a Weighted Symbolic Complexity Graph. The first two approximate reliance on rare factual knowledge and entity-heavy prompts; the last one is meant to capture symbolic and sequential inferential burden. Importantly, the authors choose interpretable features rather than opaque embedding regressors. Third is dimensionality and invariance testing. Using the benchmark designers’ predefined STEM vs non-STEM partition, they fit a confirmatory two-dimensional IRT model in which each item loads only on the latent dimension associated with its assigned partition, compare it against a unidimensional model on held-out prediction, and test whether the feature-to-difficulty mapping is the same across the two partitions. The statistical machinery includes joint Wald tests, subject-clustered covariance estimates, wild cluster bootstrap, and Holm correction, which shows care about false positives induced by subject clustering. The key technical claim is that if a benchmark truly measures one latent ability, the relationship between item difficulty and structural features should be approximately invariant across subdomains; MMLU fails that test.28:T701,The empirical setup is substantial. On the model side, the study covers 1,000 open-weight models, reduced to 869 distinct checkpoints and 50 base-family-and-size combinations after deduplication, spanning 3.2M to over 180B parameters. On the benchmark side, it uses all 14,042 MMLU test items, for about 14 million observations. After IRT calibration, item difficulty ranges from -5.00 to 7.12 with standard deviation 2.24, while model ability ranges from -2.02 to 3.14 with standard deviation 1.03. Reliability is strong: calibrating two random cohorts of 500 models separately yields a correlation of r_hh = 0.9921 between the two item-difficulty vectors. The main results are fourfold. First, the two-dimensional IRT model outperforms the unidimensional one, and the latent correlation between STEM and non-STEM ability is rho = 0.965, clearly below 1, with no posterior mass above 0.99. Second, the disattenuated subscale correlation at matched test length is 0.977, again indicating high relatedness but not interchangeability. Third, the mapping from structural features to item difficulty is not invariant across STEM and non-STEM. Among nine tested contrasts, only Entity Density survives subject-clustered covariance, wild cluster bootstrap, and Holm correction, and even that feature flips role: it behaves as an active constraint inside STEM items and as a retrieval cue outside them. Fourth, aggregate rank tracks non-STEM accuracy more closely than STEM accuracy even after equalizing test length, which is important because it shows the bias is not just a consequence of partition size. The paper also connects these findings to prior observations that chain-of-thought helps little on MMLU but more on MMLU-Pro, turning an empirical hunch into a quantified measurement argument.29:T4f0,This is a skim paper, but it is worth reading beyond the abstract. Start with the introduction, which frames the question carefully: not whether reasoning exists, but whether a single benchmark score can be interpreted as a single ability. Then read the methodology section on the 2PL setup, why SVI is used instead of JML, and how each structural feature is meant to correspond to retrieval or reasoning load. After that, focus on the comparison between one-dimensional and two-dimensional IRT, and on the invariance tests for the feature-to-difficulty mapping. Those sections carry the real evidence. The most useful figures and tables are the distributions of item difficulty and model ability, the latent correlation between the two dimensions, the regression coefficients split by STEM vs non-STEM, and the relationship between aggregate rank and partition-specific accuracy. Around 30 to 45 minutes should be enough to capture the main argument. If reading more deeply, two follow-up questions are worth keeping in mind: whether a finer subject partition would further weaken the unidimensional assumption, and whether text-level structural features are only proxies for retrieval and reasoning rather than a full account of the model-internal decomposition.2a:T47b,RAP builds a temporally grounded distribution-forecasting benchmark. The corpus starts from 356,357 AI/ML-oriented arXiv papers first submitted between 2022-06-01 and 2026-06-30 with cs.CL, cs.LG, cs.AI, or cs.CV tags. A structured extractor proposes field labels, which are normalized, expanded through prototype matching, and conservatively merged. Fields with at least 300 pre-2026-01-01 paper-field memberships are retained, yielding 278 valid fields and 1,390 episodes. For each field, a frozen codebook of exactly eight directions is built from pre-2024 evidence, with names, definitions, inclusion and exclusion rules, and exemplars. At each rolling origin, the agent can search only pre-cutoff literature and must predict the distribution of first submissions in the next six months. The evaluation compares Closed, Fixed-window Search, and Expanding-history Search. It also introduces a matched State query whose carry-forward acts as an agent-specific persistence control. Frozen-evidence replay, exact-history interventions, and outcome-aligned finetuning are used to decompose acquisition, state recovery, and future-specific updating.2b:T46f,The benchmark contains 278 AI/ML fields, five semiannual origins, and 1,390 outcome-grounded episodes. Search generally improves over Closed, but none of the four diagnostic models in natural agent settings beats an exact-count exponentially weighted moving average baseline in compositional accuracy. Under Expanding-history access, State carry-forward outperforms direct Forecast for all four models, showing that asking the agent to forecast can be worse than asking it to recover the current field state and carrying that state forward. Frozen-evidence replay attributes part of the failure to acquisition: Forecast-oriented policies allocate a smaller share of retrieved evidence to the most recent six-month window, even though that window is more useful for the future target. With exact history, future-specific updating remains limited; only GPT-5.5 with reopened Search slightly surpasses EWMA. Outcome-aligned finetuning improves Qwen3-4B by +0.105 on later-origin episodes from dependency-disjoint test fields, indicating learnability within RAP without establishing that the evidence-acquisition failure has been repaired.2c:T74a,The core contribution is a benchmark and a probing protocol, not a new model. MotionBlind uses a contrastive minimal-pair design. Each instance contains two near-identical clips recorded in the same scene with the same objects and background, differing only in one motion property such as left vs right movement, faster vs slower motion, or larger vs smaller displacement. Each clip is paired with two complementary yes/no questions, yielding four binary decisions per instance. An instance counts as correct only if all four are correct. This strict instance-level metric is intended to suppress inflated scores from partial guessing.

The dataset is self-recorded rather than scraped from the internet. That choice matters because internet videos are hard to control for static confounds and hard to label precisely for physical motion variables. The paper also prefers relative-motion questions over absolute notions like “fast” or “slow,” since absolute labels depend heavily on context. Comparing two moving references within the same scene reduces semantic ambiguity and makes the benchmark a cleaner test of motion perception.

The probing setup is well designed. First, a no-video text-only probe tests whether questions can be answered from language priors alone. Second, shuffled-frame and reversed-frame probes test temporal-order sensitivity: if performance barely changes when order is destroyed, the model is likely not using motion information. Third, the paper studies frame selection under budgets from 1 to 24 frames, comparing uniform sampling, random sampling, a GRPO-trained learned selector (HORNet), and a training-free key-clip selector (Frame2Clip). This helps separate several possible failure modes: whether one frame is already enough, whether more frames help, and whether better frame selection can recover motion understanding.2d:T615,The empirical conclusion is straightforward: current Video-LLMs perform poorly on physically grounded motion understanding, and standard video-QA scores do not reveal this weakness. The paper evaluates models on MotionBlind together with TimeBlind and Video-MME. Non-contrastive benchmarks such as Video-MME can make models look broadly capable, but performance drops sharply when static content is controlled and only motion differs. The introduction cites TimeBlind as a reference point, where even strong frontier models reach only 48.2% instance accuracy while humans achieve 98.2%. MotionBlind narrows the focus further to speed, direction, and magnitude.

The diagnostic results are the most useful part. The text-only probe checks whether benchmark leakage through language priors is possible. The shuffled/reversed-frame probes test whether models are actually sensitive to temporal order. If order corruption causes only limited degradation, that is strong evidence that the model is reading static frames rather than motion. The frame-budget and frame-selection experiments then ask whether the issue is simply missing key frames. The paper’s answer is largely no: increasing frames or using more sophisticated selectors does not fully recover the missing capability. The excerpt provided here does not include the full result tables for MotionBlind, so I will not fabricate exact numbers. But the paper’s argument is clear: the bottleneck is deeper than frame sampling and points to weak motion representations in current Video-LLM pipelines.2e:T496,This is a good skim paper. Read it for the benchmark design and the diagnostic logic, not for model innovation. Start with the introduction to understand why standard video-QA can overestimate temporal competence. Then focus on the dataset construction principles: self-recorded clips, minimal pairs, relative-motion questions, the 2x2 contrastive protocol, and strict instance-level scoring. That is the conceptual core of the paper.

In the experiments, read by probe type rather than by model. Start with the no-video probe, then the shuffled/reversed-frame tests, and finally the frame-selection and frame-budget results. That order makes the argument easier to follow: is the task solvable from language alone, does temporal order matter, and can better frame ingestion fix the problem? A 25–35 minute read is enough to capture the main point. If going deeper, useful follow-up questions are why current video pretraining objectives fail to force motion learning, whether visual tokenization loses too much temporal-difference information, and whether explicit motion supervision or world-model-style predictive objectives should be introduced earlier in pretraining.2f:T938,The framework has three main components. First, a VLM-based coding agent takes a reference image and natural-language instructions, initializes the world, identifies entities, assigns attributes and states, and writes executable programs that define interactions, triggers, and objectives. Second, a state executor maintains an explicit global world state and updates it according to user actions and programmed rules. This state includes off-screen entities and non-visual fields, so the system does not depend on the renderer to remember the world. Third, a generative renderer produces observations conditioned on compiled controls derived from the current state.

The key design choice is the intermediate representation. The paper compares text descriptions, 2D boxes and masks, detailed 3D scenes, and dense rendering controls such as G-buffers. It then chooses a state-augmented 3D oriented bounding box for each entity. The OBB encodes position, extent, and orientation in a shared world coordinate system, and is augmented with persistent identity, semantic category, dynamic state, and appearance information. This is a deliberate compromise. It is more geometric and view-consistent than text, less camera-tied than 2D controls, and much lighter than full 3D assets that would require explicit pose and geometry synthesis at inference time.

A deterministic state compiler then projects the evolving OBBs along the target camera trajectory and rasterizes them into pixel-aligned spatiotemporal controls. This is important. Instead of asking the video model to infer camera projection and entity-pixel correspondence implicitly, the compiler resolves those constraints explicitly. The renderer is left to synthesize fine geometry, articulation, texture, and secondary dynamics that are not specified by the compact state. In effect, the system splits the problem into explicit state evolution plus conditional generative completion.

The training side includes a data curation pipeline that recovers the spatial and semantic supervision needed for this interface from videos. Even from the available text, it is clear that the authors treat this pipeline as necessary for scaling: without extracting entity-level structure from video data, the proposed interface would remain a hand-built control mechanism rather than a trainable pretraining target.30:T461,The visible experimental emphasis is on state-video consistency rather than generic video quality. The paper introduces CombatStateBench, a controlled benchmark designed to test whether generated videos remain faithful to the engine-maintained world state. The benchmark focuses on combat scenarios and measures whether the number of visible alive characters is correct and whether death states are visually realized. That is a sensible choice because these are exactly the kinds of failures that expose a mismatch between explicit state execution and generative rendering.

The full text excerpt provided here does not include result tables, model sizes, training scale, or exact gains, so it would be unsafe to invent numbers. Still, the right way to read the experiments is not just by average scores. The more informative questions are: when does the renderer fail to obey the compiled controls, when does the coding agent initialize the wrong state, and how does error accumulate over long interactions. Those failure modes matter more than a single benchmark number for judging whether this decomposition is viable.31:T521,The generation process is formulated as a token-level MDP. The state is the prompt plus the generated prefix, transitions are deterministic token appends, and the verifier reward is observed only at the terminal position. Under ordinary value regression on stale trajectories, the squared-loss minimizer follows the Bellman operator of μ, so repeated critic updates converge toward V^μ. The actor, however, needs advantages based on V^π. The induced bias is position dependent: it is largest near the beginning of a response because the remaining suffix is generated under the stale policy.

BRACE modifies the value target rather than only the actor objective. It uses a k-capped correction horizon, applying importance correction only over the next k policy tokens after each position. This avoids multiplying π/μ ratios over an entire long sequence. Beyond that local window, it attaches an anchored Monte Carlo tail with a constant weight, so the terminal reward still reaches every position. The design separates local policy-mismatch correction from long-range reward propagation. This is the key difference from V-trace: with a short V-trace window, the target may contain no terminal reward; with a full-length window, the product of clipped importance ratios becomes unstable over long trajectories.32:T6b4,The core method is still directional ablation, adapted to MoE, multi-stream residuals, and FP8 weights. The basic recipe is to estimate a refusal direction from a few hundred contrastive prompts, then orthogonalize the weights that write to the residual stream against that direction so the model can no longer express refusal along it. The technical difficulty comes from three shifts. First, in an MoE, the residual writers are no longer just attention and dense MLP projections; routed experts and shared projections also matter. The paper shows that if one reuses the old dense-model implementation based on module-name traversal, many fused expert parameters are never touched. The edit appears to run cleanly but changes almost nothing, which creates a silent-failure mode. Second, GLM-5.3-Flash uses a four-wide hyper-connection residual. The authors argue that this topology does not fundamentally block directional ablation because the stream-mixing weights are scalar: if a direction is zeroed in every stream, later mixing keeps it zero. This is why directly editing residual-writing weights is more complete than hooking layer outputs. Third, the paper tests whether the single-direction hypothesis survives at frontier scale. It does not rely only on rank-1 ablation; it also tries higher-rank fitted subspaces and uses random orthogonal directions as controls. The main technical conclusion is twofold: the editable part of refusal is distributed across attention, dense/shared projections, and routed experts, and a nontrivial residue remains even after low-rank subspace removal, concentrated in a few content categories. So refusal is not fully captured by one simple linear direction in this model.33:T684,The target model is GLM-5.3-Flash: 320B parameters, 288 routed experts, a four-stream hyper-connection residual, and block-FP8 weights. The most informative experiment decomposes the edit effect across all subsets of writer groups. The result is strongly non-additive. Editing attention alone, dense/shared projections alone, or routed experts alone removes only 0.039, 0.016, and 0.148 of refusal, respectively. Editing all three together removes 0.776. In other words, roughly three quarters of the removable effect appears only under joint editing, not within any single module family. That directly argues against a simple picture in which refusal is localized to one component type. The paper also quantifies the MoE implementation pitfall: the conventional name-matching recipe reaches only a subset equivalent to 0.066 out of the available 0.776, so on this architecture it mostly misses the target while looking successful. Another comparison shows that directly editing residual-writing weights is more thorough than boundary hooking. To verify specificity, the authors ablate random directions orthogonal to the refusal direction and observe essentially no refusal removal. On seven harmful benchmarks, the method reduces refusal by 41 to 89 percentage points with no detected capability loss. The spread is itself informative: the weaker gains occur on benchmarks enriched with resistant categories. The authors then fit category-specific subspaces up to rank 12 and still observe residual refusal, suggesting that in this model safety alignment is not fully removable by a single direction, and not even fully removable by a small low-rank linear subspace.34:T511,This is a skim paper, but it rewards reading the main body. Start with the introduction, which clearly lays out the three assumption shifts from prior work: dense to MoE, single-stream to multi-stream residuals, and standard precision to FP8. Then read the method section on what counts as a residual writer in this architecture and why module-name matching misses fused experts. After that, go straight to the writer-group subset decomposition; this is the most informative result in the paper. If you only inspect one table or figure, inspect that one. Next, read the analysis of hyper-connection residuals to understand why multi-stream topology does not invalidate the attack in principle. Finally, read the experiments on resistant categories and higher-rank subspaces, because they keep the conclusion measured: directional ablation removes a large fraction of refusal, but not all of it. Around 25 to 40 minutes should be enough for the main line. If reading more deeply, three follow-up questions are worth pursuing: whether the resistant residue reflects deeper reward-model imprinting, whether refusal in MoE models is stored inside expert representations or in attention-expert interaction, and whether low-rank editability decreases as post-training becomes stronger or RL runs longer.35:T7bf,The method is deliberately simple but well targeted. The authors define two axes. Direct rate (DR) measures how often the model repeats its top answer over 30 samples of the original prompt at temperature 0.6. This captures concentration under one wording. Framed rate (FR) then asks whether that same direct top answer survives 30 matched scenario reframings of the underlying question. Importantly, FR is not the concentration of answers under reframing; it is the reappearance rate of the original direct top answer. That design choice is the core of the paper, because it measures persistence of a specific preference rather than generic consistency. The final bias depth score is π = DR × FR, which is high only when the model is both concentrated under direct prompting and robust to reframing.

The dataset construction is also central. They build 4,442 opinion prompt families, each grounded in a real Olmo-3-7B-SFT training example, and create matched everyday-scenario reframings for each family. Because the task is open-ended generation rather than multiple choice, answer normalization is necessary to merge semantically equivalent outputs and avoid contaminating FR with superficial phrasing variation. The contribution is therefore less about a new model and more about turning a causally meaningful question—does a preference survive paraphrastic and situational variation?—into a scalable evaluation protocol.

The paper then runs this protocol across the full Olmo-3-7B release pipeline: Pretrained → SFT → DPO → RLVR. That allows stage-wise tracking of bias depth. It also adds two attribution analyses: whether the post-SFT top answer matches the pretrained top answer, and whether a biased answer can be traced back to a specific SFT training example. Finally, it tests removability using continued LoRA-SFT and prompt-based debiasing with GEPA, asking whether Deep and Shallow biases differ not just in origin but also in how hard they are to erase.36:T5c3,There are four results that matter most. First, across four post-SFT models, 35.8% to 77.7% of the 4,442 prompt families look biased under direct prompting, but only about a quarter of those direct biases survive reframing. So most of what a single-prompt protocol would count as bias is shallow. Second, along the full Olmo-3-7B pipeline, most Deep bias is already present at the pretrained stage, whereas Shallow bias keeps increasing through SFT and every later post-training stage. That is the clearest empirical support for the paper’s main interpretation. Third, biases that survive reframing are much more likely to match the pretrained model’s top answer, suggesting inheritance from pretraining rather than creation by SFT alone. At the same time, clean attribution to one specific SFT example works only for a small minority of cases, which argues against a simplistic single-example memorization story and points instead to broader distributional effects. Fourth, debiasing is asymmetric: continued LoRA-SFT reduces Deep bias by only 4.5 percentage points versus 10.3 points for Shallow bias, and GEPA reduces Deep bias by 1.6 points versus 5.8 points for Shallow. Those removability gaps line up with the stage-of-origin analysis.

For a pretraining reader, the main signal is not any one absolute score but the consistency of the whole picture: biases with stronger evidence of pretraining inheritance are more robust to reframing and harder to remove later.37:T483,This is a skim paper, but a careful skim is worthwhile. Start with the introduction and Section 2 to internalize the DR/FR split. The key detail is that FR tracks recurrence of the direct top answer under reframing, not the top answer under each reframed prompt. Without that detail, the paper can look like a generic robustness study, which it is not. Then read the dataset section closely enough to understand how prompt families, scenario reframings, and answer normalization are built; the validity of the Deep/Shallow distinction depends heavily on these choices. In the experiments, prioritize the stage-wise analysis, the pretrained-versus-SFT attribution, and the debiasing section. When looking at figures, focus on whether the same answer persists across checkpoints and how much each debiasing method moves Deep versus Shallow cases.

A solid read takes about 25–35 minutes. If your work touches data or alignment, the two parts worth deeper inspection are the open-ended answer matching procedure and the cases that cannot be traced to a single SFT example, since those are the ones most suggestive of distribution-level pretraining effects.38:T40b,HEAL first applies causal noise intervention to multi-head attention outputs. Instead of inferring head importance from attention weights or output projection weights, it perturbs head outputs directly and measures the resulting change in the layer representation. This is a better intervention point because a head with small projection weights can still matter if its activation is large, and a head with large weights can be inactive in a given context. After filtering causally redundant heads, the method uses counterfactual Difference-in-Differences to disentangle visual and language information at the head level. The remaining heads are categorized as redundant, visual, language, or synergy heads. The central observation is that hallucination is associated with distributional drift inside synergy heads, not simply with weak visual heads or strong language heads. HEAL then introduces a dynamic calibration rule with an equilibrium factor α to adjust the visual-language information balance during autoregressive decoding.39:T44f,The protocol represents each behavioral evaluation as a set of conditions with measured rates. A condition may be an MMLU subject in a sycophancy test, a decision-topic and demographic cell in a discrimination evaluation, or an agentic scenario. A prediction method outputs one rate per condition, and performance is scored by correlation and calibration on frozen splits. The paper varies three axes independently: information available to the model, from abstract descriptions to exact items and behavior history; the subject of the prompt, including “you,” a named model, and “capable AI agents in general”; and the question format, either direct rate prediction or forced choice. The crucial design choice is the subject control. If self-directed prompts perform no better than generic-agent prompts, positive correlations cannot be interpreted as privileged self-knowledge. Additional controls include noise ceilings, cross-model consensus, predictions from other models’ measured behavior, other models’ answers about themselves, identity transfer, and re-elicitation after finetuning.3a:T599,The study covers nine public behavioral evaluations, 15 prediction methods, and 12 models from six labs, including frontier models with and without reasoning. Direct self-report has almost no predictive power for the reporting model’s behavior, with r=+0.04. Showing the model its own measured history raises this to +0.19, and showing exact evaluation items raises it to +0.24. However, the same item-informed question about capable AI agents in general reaches +0.28, and other models’ self-reports predict the target model at least as well as the target model’s own answers, with +0.35 versus +0.24 in the reported comparison. Each model’s answers fit other models’ behavior nearly as well as its own; the self-advantage is only +0.02. The paper also estimates how much reliable variance is model-specific: about 0.04 for capability-style evaluations, where models mostly agree on what is hard, and about 0.97 for agentic policy evaluations, where models differ more. Scaling improves predictions about AI assistants in general, but not identification of the model’s own tendencies. Naming the model induces a self-flattering shift: on harmful behaviors, self-reports are about 0.15 lower than generic-agent reports. Finetuning on records of the model’s own behavior helps narrowly on the trained behavior, but it also changes the behavior being predicted and transfers poorly across behaviors or abstraction levels.3b:T852,The method, State-Path Tool Menu, has three components: an encoder, a retriever, and a reranker. Each tool has documentation, required inputs, and produced outputs. A request exposes an initial state s0(q), containing the fields and constraints visible before any tool call. The system must construct an ordered menu of K=32 tools, and the online agent can only use tools from that menu during execution.

The central idea is the state path, a latent pre-execution route from the observable initial state to the desired outcome. Tools on this route are assigned one of four roles: entry tools can run immediately from the initial state, bridge tools create intermediate fields needed later, the target tool resolves the main request, and an optional terminal tool commits or delivers the result. This role decomposition is useful because it separates different notions that relevance-based retrieval conflates: immediate executability, intermediate utility, and final-task alignment.

The encoder learns relation-aware tool representations from the request state, tool schemas, and successful training trajectories. The excerpt explicitly mentions three directional signals: whether a tool can run now, whether it produces fields needed later, and how it depends on other tools in precedence order. So the representation is not just semantic similarity over tool descriptions; it is structured around state compatibility and dependency. The retriever then selects 32 tools that cover complementary path roles rather than simply maximizing top-k relevance. The reranker orders the selected tools so that producers appear before consumers, making the menu easier to execute under the same online loop and call budget.

Training supervision comes from successful trajectories. The retriever is trained with route membership, entry, and path-role targets, while the reranker is trained with slot and pairwise precedence targets. A notable design choice is that the paper keeps the online agent unchanged. The gains are meant to come from better pre-execution menu construction, not from modifying the planner or increasing the budget.3c:T4b9,From the available text, the experiments compare State-Path menus against relevance-based menus under a fixed agent and a fixed 32-tool budget. The reported evaluation dimensions include end-task success, complete-chain coverage, and executable entry. The paper also claims comparisons across tool libraries, evaluators, and executor families, plus paired outcomes and controlled variants to isolate whether the gains come from route coverage and ordering.

The provided excerpt does not include the result tables, exact gains, dataset sizes, or training details, so it would be inappropriate to fabricate numbers. Even so, the experimental design is already informative. The control variables are tight: the online execution loop and call budget remain unchanged. The metrics are also more diagnostic than standard success rate alone, because they measure whether the menu contains a complete chain and whether executable tools appear early enough to be useful. When reading the full paper, the most important checks are whether the gains are larger on long dependency chains, whether the method is more robust in larger tool libraries, and whether reranking adds substantial value beyond retrieval coverage.3d:T416,This is a skim paper. Start with the introduction, especially the receipt and email example, because it makes the failure mode concrete: the final tool is easy to retrieve, but the bridge tools determine whether the route is executable. Then read the first half of the methodology section to understand the state-path abstraction, the four tool roles, and why menu order matters before execution begins. After that, go to the main experimental tables and ablations to see whether the gains come mostly from relation-aware retrieval, path-role coverage, or producer-before-consumer ordering.

A focused read should take about 20 to 30 minutes. If you work on agents, the appendices on visible information at each stage, trajectory supervision, and case studies are worth extra time because they determine how realistic the setup is for real API ecosystems. high-dimensional linear regression when real and synthetic data share the same covariate distribution but have shifted labeling functions.","importance":"The useful lesson for pretraining is not the linear-regression model itself, but the separation between data mixture and data schedule. A fixed fraction of synthetic data in a mixed stream can create a non-vanishing excess-risk floor, while a synthetic-then-real curriculum can remove that floor. This supports a practical intuition in LLM training: synthetic data quality and ratio matter, but the stage at which synthetic data enters the run can matter just as much.","technical_approach":"The analysis compares two protocols for last-iterate one-pass SGD. In mixed training, each update is drawn from either the real source or the synthetic source. In two-stage training, the model is first trained on synthetic data and then updated on real data. The risk bounds decompose ordinary bias and variance from mismatch-induced terms. For mixed training, the mismatch appears as fluctuation from random source switching and persistent drift from the shifted synthetic labeling function. For two-stage training, the synthetic phase mainly contributes a filtered initialization bias that can be washed out by the real-data phase. The paper also develops scaling-law-style bounds under a random sketch model, characterizing when larger models amplify synthetic-induced degradation and when high-quality synthetic data reduces bias.","experiments":"The contribution is theoretical. The main finite-sample upper and lower bounds show strong model collapse for mixed training: with a fixed synthetic-data fraction, the excess risk on the real distribution has a non-vanishing floor under data scaling. The same qualitative behavior is shown for iterate-averaged SGD, so the effect is not just a last-iterate artifact. Two-stage training avoids this floor and can achieve vanishing excess risk. Under the random sketch model, the mixed-training bound is tight in an optimization-saturated regime, and the analysis gives an exact finite-sample condition under which two-stage training strictly improves over real-only training with the same real-data budget and real-stage updates.","reading_guide":"Skim in 30–45 minutes. Focus on Section 4.1 for the mixed-training decomposition into fluctuation and drift, Section 4.2 for why two-stage training avoids the excess-risk floor, Section 4.3 for the random-sketch scaling behavior, and Section 4.4 for the finite-sample condition under which synthetic pretraining helps. The most relevant takeaway for LLM pretraining is the optimizer-level distinction between continuously mixing biased synthetic data and using it as an early-stage curriculum.","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"从理论上证明了合成数据预训练中 '先合成后真实' 的两阶段策略能避免模型崩溃，对 data curriculum 设计有直接指导意义。","en":"Theoretically proves that a two-stage 'synthetic then real' strategy in pre-training can avoid model collapse, offering direct guidance for data curriculum design."},"metadata":{"institutions":[],"authors":["Jichu li","Difan Zou"],"notable_authors":[],"tags":["Synthetic Data","Model Collapse","Data Curriculum","SGD","High-Dimensional Linear Regression"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09572","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.09572","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2609.09848","title":"Subgroup Membership Inference Audits of Differentially Private Synthetic Text","abstract":"Synthetic data releases are increasingly proposed in the literature as a means of sharing realistic data replicas in lieu of sensitive private datasets. Even when the worst-case privacy leakage of such releases is bounded by means of differential privacy (DP), in practice a residual risk remains. Membership inference attack (MIA) audits are conducted to empirically quantify this risk. However, existing methods only measure average-case risk for randomly drawn records, which might conceal the ris","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-09-09T07:56:24Z","category_id":"pretraining_data","cover_url":"/covers/2609_09848.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CR","cs.AI"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作处理的不是“DP 合成文本是否平均上安全”，而是一个更尖锐的问题：在差分隐私已经给出最坏情况上界之后，真实风险是否会集中落在某些更脆弱的子群体上，而传统平均化 membership inference audit 会把这件事掩盖掉。作者把 target pool 显式纳入 membership inference game，要求攻击者不是对随机样本做平均审计，而是对预先定义的子群体做定向审计。这样一来，问题从“整体 AUC 是否下降”变成了“哪些记录、哪些群体、在什么攻击知识条件下仍然可辨识”。对合成文本尤其重要，因为文本记录的独特性、稀有表达和语义离群性都可能让泄露高度不均匀。","importance":"对预训练和数据研究的意义主要有三层。第一，它提醒人们不要把 DP 或 synthetic release 的平均风险当成充分结论；如果高风险样本集中在少量记录上，平均指标会低估真实隐私代价。第二，它把“记录可记忆性/可辨识性”从模型侧问题推进到数据侧问题：哪些文本更容易在生成后留下痕迹，和数据分布尾部、独特性、群体结构直接相关。这对预训练数据筛选、去重、长尾控制、敏感样本识别都有启发。第三，它给 release-based 审计提供了更现实的框架。很多已有 MIA 依赖访问生成器或训练过程，不适合真实数据发布场景；这里把攻击者知识分层，并把 proxy evidence 与 calibration 分开，方法上更接近实际部署时能做的审计。贡献量级不是提出新的生成模型，而是把隐私评估从平均视角推进到 subgroup-aware、record-level 的细粒度视角，这对数据治理和合成数据发布规范是实打实的增量。","technical_approach":"方法上有三个关键点。第一，作者形式化了 subgroup-targeted membership inference game。目标记录不是从全体样本随机抽，而是从一个显式 target pool 中抽取；训练其余私有数据时从补集采样。这个设计允许把“高风险子群体”“随机对照组”“多个子群体并到同一参考分布下比较”统一到一个框架里。第二，作者把 normalized-evidence MIA 适配到 synthetic text release。由于对整个 release dataset 做精确 likelihood-ratio test 不可计算，他们用可测的 proxy score 近似证据强度，比如 n-gram overlap、embedding distance 等，再根据攻击者可获得的信息做 scenario-dependent calibration。这里的拆分很重要：proxy 只依赖发布出来的合成文本，calibration 则编码攻击者知道多少背景信息。第三，作者把攻击能力明确分成三档：release-only attacker、raw-reference attacker、generator-assisted release attacker。这样可以区分“仅看发布数据能推断多少”“额外原始参考语料能带来多少”“若攻击者还能接触生成器，风险又会抬高多少”。此外，他们不仅报告 pool-level aggregate metric，还通过重复 attack episodes 给出 per-record vulnerability score，并尝试用记录内在属性预测哪些样本更脆弱。这一点很像把隐私审计做成了数据质量画像，而不只是一个单一 AUC 数字。","experiments":"实验覆盖四个数据集、三种 DP 文本生成器、五个 privacy budget，并在 32 个 proxy 上做审计。结果里最重要的不是“DP 有效”这句，而是“DP 有效但不均匀”。在非私有生成器上，针对高风险记录，最强攻击者的 AUC 达到 0.79–0.83；在 FPR 低于 5% 时，可识别 34–61% 的真实成员。即便攻击者只能看到 release，本身也能识别 39–52% 的真实成员，说明单靠发布样本就可能泄露不少信息。加入 DP 后，平均 AUC 明显下降，但所有测试的 privacy budget 下仍高于随机：AUC 约 0.55–0.65，在 5% FPR 下 TPR 最高仍接近 18%。更关键的是，DP 对不同 pool 的保护并不均匀：高风险 pool 在每个 DP budget 下都比随机对照更可区分，说明 DP 没有把风险均匀压平。作者还发现风险在 pool 内部高度集中，约 10% 的记录承担了约 40% 的总泄露。这个结果对数据筛选很有启发：如果能在发布前识别这部分记录，可能比单纯继续压 ε 更有效。全文还比较了不同 proxy、不同攻击知识条件、不同机制下的差异，结论是泄露结构既依赖数据分布，也依赖生成机制，不能只看一个统一平均数。","reading_guide":"泛读时先抓三处。第一，看 Introduction 和 problem setup，弄清 target pool 这个参数化游戏为什么比随机抽样审计更贴近真实风险。第二，看方法部分里 proxy evidence 与 calibration 的拆分，这是整篇最有方法味道的地方；如果只记一个点，就记住“同一类 release-based proxy 可以在不同 attacker scenario 下重校准”。第三，看结果图里 pool-level AUC/TPR 与 per-record vulnerability 的分布，尤其是高风险 pool 与随机 pool 在 DP 前后差异是否缩小。预计 25–35 分钟能把主线读清。若你做预训练数据治理，值得多看作者如何定义高风险 pool、如何分析记录属性与 vulnerability 的关系，因为这部分最容易迁移到去重、敏感样本检测和数据配比策略里。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper asks a sharper question than standard privacy auditing: after differential privacy is applied to synthetic text generation, is the remaining membership leakage evenly reduced, or does it stay concentrated on vulnerable subgroups and distinctive records? Existing audits mostly estimate average-case membership inference risk over randomly sampled targets. That can hide concentrated failures. The authors therefore define a subgroup-targeted membership inference game where the target pool is an explicit parameter, so the audit can focus on records with specific properties rather than reporting a single population-wide average.","importance":"Its value for pretraining and data research is mostly on the data-governance side. First, it shows that average privacy metrics can be misleading when leakage is concentrated in the tail of the data distribution. Second, it reframes privacy risk as a record-level and subgroup-level property tied to distinctiveness, rarity, and distributional structure, which is directly relevant to deduplication, sensitive-data filtering, and long-tail data curation. Third, it provides a release-based audit framework that is more realistic for actual synthetic-data sharing than attacks requiring generator access. The contribution is not a new generative model; it is a better way to measure where privacy failures remain after DP, and that matters for any pipeline that treats synthetic text as a safer substitute for raw training data.","technical_approach":"$17","experiments":"$18","reading_guide":"For a quick read, focus on three parts. 增益；二是推理能力不能只靠后训练硬拉，必须在预训练数据配比、中期激活、RL 单位设计上形成连续演化链条。","importance":"对预训练研究的意义主要有四层。第一，它给出了一套面向 trillion-scale 稀疏模型的工程化 scaling recipe，不只是报一个大模型结果，而是把小规模试验如何外推到 1e25 FLOPs 级训练讲得比较完整。第二，它把 reasoning-oriented pretraining 明确写成数据课程问题：20T token 过程中逐步提高数学和代码等推理数据占比，而不是静态 mixture。第三，它把 mid-training 放到很核心的位置，用长上下文和 CoT 数据做 reasoning pre-activation，说明 pretrain 与 post-train 之间存在一个值得单独设计的接口层。第四，它提出 WSM、LPO、GAR、全 FP8、异构 pipeline 等一组配套机制，显示出大模型能力提升越来越依赖训练目标、调度器、RL action granularity 与系统 co-design 的联动。若这些结果可复现，这篇工作更像一份大规模开源 reasoning foundation 的 recipe 文档，而不只是模型发布。","technical_approach":"$19","experiments":"全文给出的关键数字不少。模型族覆盖 16B、若干中间规模到 1T total parameters，统一采用高稀疏 MoE；每 token 仅激活约 3.5% 参数，作者称相对 dense counterpart 可获得最高 7× active-compute efficiency。预训练总量为 20T tokens，reasoning-oriented 数据占比从 32% 逐步升至 46%，仅这一数据策略就在 reasoning benchmarks 上带来平均 5%–8% 提升。WSM 调度器相对传统学习率衰减方案带来平均 1%–2% 增益，而且优势能保留到后续 post-training。DFT + Evo-CoT 使模型在竞赛级数学推理 benchmark 上超过对比模型，同时达到相当或更好性能所需训练 token 减少 25%。LPO 相比 token-level 和 sequence-level RL 基线，在复杂推理 benchmark 上约有 10% 提升。GAR 在开放式评测中的一致性分数提升 2%–10%。系统层面，全 FP8 训练在 900B tokens 后与 BF16 的精度差距不超过 0.25%，同时内存占用下降超过 15%；异构 pipeline 设计使吞吐提升约 40%。这些数字里，最值得认真看的是数据课程、WSM、LPO、FP8 这四组，因为它们分别对应 pretrain、mid/post-train 接口、RL credit assignment、训练系统稳定性四个不同层面的可迁移经验。","reading_guide":"精读时建议按“先总框架，再抓可迁移部件”的顺序看。第一遍先看 Introduction 和总览图，确认作者把能力提升拆成 architecture、data、mid-training、post-training、infrastructure 五条线，而不是把所有收益都归因于模型规模。第二遍重点看 Ling Scaling Laws 相关章节和图表，关注他们如何定义 active compute、如何做小模型到 1T 的外推、哪些超参被证明跨尺度稳定。这里决定这篇工作到底是可复用 recipe，还是一次性工程堆叠。第三遍看 pretraining data composition 与 mid-training 章节，尤其是 20T token 中 reasoning 数据比例的调度方式、长上下文与 CoT 预激活的插入时机，以及这些设计为什么能提高后续 RL 上限。第四遍看 WSM scheduler、DFT/Evo-CoT、LPO、GAR 的消融，重点不是记住名字，而是理解它们分别在解决什么训练病灶：学习率后期退火效率低、初始化单一、token 级 credit 太碎、偏好比较粒度太粗。最后看系统章节，尤其是 FP8 精度配置和异构 pipeline 调度，因为这些细节决定大规模 recipe 是否真的可落地。若时间有限，优先看 scaling law 图、数据配比消融、WSM 对比、LPO 对比、FP8 与 BF16 gap 曲线，预计 60–90 分钟能抓住主干；若要复现实验思路，系统和训练调度部分需要再花一轮时间。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper tackles a full-stack scaling problem rather than a single modeling trick: how to build an open trillion-parameter reasoning-oriented foundation model that remains compute-efficient, trainable, and predictably improvable, while preserving reasoning gains from pre-training through mid-training and post-training. The central tension is clear throughout the paper: sparse MoE scaling is only useful if the saved active compute converts into better reasoning, and reasoning cannot be treated as a purely post-training artifact. It has to be shaped by data mixture, intermediate activation stages, and RL design choices.","importance":"Its value for pretraining research comes from four directions. Second, it treats reasoning-oriented pretraining as a curriculum over data composition, not a fixed mixture: math and code proportions are increased over a 20T-token run. Third, it elevates mid-training into a distinct interface between base pretraining and alignment, using long-context and CoT data to pre-activate reasoning before SFT/RL. Fourth, it couples modeling, scheduling, RL action granularity, and systems design in one stack: WSM, LPO, GAR, full FP8, and heterogeneous pipeline scheduling are presented as mutually reinforcing pieces. If the claims hold, this is less a model announcement and more a reusable recipe for large open reasoning While standard pre-training loss compares model outputs to realized future values, SDD compares outputs to the conditional forecast distribution of the trajectory. This corresponds to a Rao-Blackwellization of the training objective, provably reducing the covariance of the stochastic gradient under the Loewner partial ordering while keeping its expectation unchanged.","experiments":"Validated SDD on Time Series Foundation Models (TSFMs) ranging from 4M to 2.5B parameters. decoding 的收益。第二步，在通用语料上用 next-token prediction 继续训练这个裁剪后的模型，恢复被裁剪后损失的语言建模能力；这一阶段不依赖任何 target model。第三步，面向具体 target model 替换 tokenizer，并对 embedding 和 LM head 做词表适配。第四步，引入 target hidden states：通过给 QKV projection 扩展零初始化列，使模型能接收 target activation，同时不破坏已有预训练初始化；随后用 target logits 做蒸馏，让 drafter 更贴近 target distribution。与 EAGLE 类方法相比，Osprey 的关键差异是把通用语言先验和 target 对齐解耦，而不是从一开始就绑定到 target representation space。","experiments":"论文用 workload shift 展示现有 drafter 的脆弱性：例如 Eagle3 在数学数据上训练时，数学域平均 acceptance length 为 5.06，但迁移到代码数据后只有 1.86。这个结果说明 acceptance length 对训练分布很敏感。Osprey 的实验重点应是比较 target-agnostic 预训练 backbone 与 target-specific drafter 在不同任务分布、不同 target model 上的 acceptance length 和端到端 speedup。给出的正文片段没有包含完整实验表格，因此不应过度解读具体提升幅度；可确定的是，论文把“跨分布稳定 acceptance”作为核心指标，而不是只看单一域内加速。","reading_guide":"建议泛读 Introduction 和方法图，重点看四阶段 training recipe。实验部分优先看三类结果：一是 in-domain 与 out-of-domain acceptance length 的差异；二是裁剪层数、target-agnostic 预训练、zero-init QKV 扩展各自的消融；三是不同 target tokenizer / target architecture 下复用同一 backbone 的结果。预计 25–35 分钟可以抓住主要贡献；若关心 serving 落地，需要细看 latency 分解，确认 drafter 额外计算是否真的被 acceptance gain 覆盖。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"Osprey addresses a robustness issue in speculative decoding: drafters trained for a specific target model and a narrow workload can lose acceptance rate sharply when the serving distribution shifts, which directly erodes decoding speedup.","importance":"The useful idea for pretraining researchers is the separation between a reusable language prior and target-specific alignment. The paper treats the drafter as a small pretrained language model rather than a narrowly trained serving component. This is not a new language-modeling objective, but it is a concrete example of how pretrained small-model capability can be repurposed inside inference systems.","technical_approach":"The recipe has four stages. First, an off-the-shelf small LM is pruned down to its embeddings, LM head, and a small number of early Transformer layers, producing a shallow backbone suitable for drafting. Second, the pruned model is trained with next-token prediction on a broad corpus to recover general language-modeling ability; this stage is independent of any target model. Third, the checkpoint is adapted to a target model by replacing the tokenizer and aligning the embedding and output head with the target vocabulary. Fourth, the model is conditioned on target hidden states and distilled toward target logits. The QKV projections are expanded with zero-initialized columns so the drafter can ingest target activations without disturbing the pretrained initialization. Compared with EAGLE-style target-specific drafters, the main distinction is that broad pretraining is done once and target alignment is handled later.","experiments":"The paper motivates the problem with a clear domain-shift result: an Eagle3 drafter trained on math reaches an average acceptance length of 5.06 on math data but drops to 1.86 on code data. This shows that acceptance length is highly sensitive to workload mismatch. ","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-09-08T06:23:07Z","category_id":"efficient","cover_url":"/covers/2609_09241.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.AI","cs.CL"],"summary":{"core_problem":"论文解决动态稀疏 输出的 RMS 主要受归一化 routing scores 的平方和控制。减少 k 后，较低分 expert 被移除，剩余分数被重新放大，平方和上升，因此加权输出的 RMS 和 variance 会增加。这个尺度放大会进入 residual stream，并通过后续层传播。基于该观察，作者提出 Layer-wise Distribution Alignment，简称 LDA。LDA 在每个 SMoE 层之后做按维度的 moment correction，把 reduced routing 下的表示对齐到默认 top-k0 路由下的 layer-wise reference distribution。它不改模型结构，不需要重新训练，可以叠加在 top-k、adaptive top-p 和 dynamic expert pruning sparse MoE inference. When a pretrained MoE model is run with fewer or dynamically selected experts than the training-time top-k configuration, the intermediate representation distribution shifts. The resulting degradation is not only due to reduced expert capacity; it also comes from systematic changes in output scale and variance.","importance":"The work is mainly about inference efficiency, but it has clear implications for MoE pretraining.
